SKILL.md:1085:| integration-results.json | pending | quality/results/ | Structured integration output |
SKILL.md:1297:- For sidecar JSON files (`tdd-results.json`, `integration-results.json`), the `skill_version` field already serves as the version stamp. JSON does not support comments — do not inject one.
SKILL.md:1359:Organize the tests into three logical groups (classes, describe blocks, modules, or whatever the test framework uses):
SKILL.md:1609:**Per-UC group splitting (mandatory).** Each integration test group must map to at most **2 use cases**. A group that maps to 3+ UCs is too coarse — it can't distinguish which use case failed when a test breaks. If a single test command (e.g., `mvn test`, `go test ./...`) would exercise multiple use cases, split it into separate groups with targeted test selectors (`-Dtest=`, `-run`, `-k`, `--tests`, `-- test_name`, etc.) so each group isolates 1–2 UCs. Groups covering all UCs in one undifferentiated command are explicitly prohibited — they provide no diagnostic value when a failure occurs.
SKILL.md:1611:**No-selector fallback.** If the project's test framework cannot select tests at the granularity needed for splitting (e.g., a monolithic test suite with no tag/filter support), document the limitation in the integration protocol and use the narrowest feasible command. Record which UCs the group covers and why further splitting is not possible. **A single-command project must still use the grouped JSON schema** — wrap the command in one group with a `use_cases` list covering all UCs that command exercises. A flat list of commands is never a valid substitute for the `groups[]` structure.
SKILL.md:1626:**Infrastructure group definition.** A single `[Infrastructure]` group may cover build validation, race detection, static analysis, and platform compatibility checks without UC mapping. Infrastructure tests verify build toolchain and platform support, not user-observable behavior. Infrastructure groups:
SKILL.md:1633:**Include an Execution UX section.** When someone tells an AI agent to "run the integration tests," the agent needs to know how to present its work. The protocol should specify three phases: (1) show the plan as a numbered table before running anything, (2) report one-line progress updates as each test runs (`✓`/`✗`/`⧗`), (3) show a summary table with pass/fail counts and a recommendation. See `references/review_protocols.md` section "Execution UX" for the template and examples. Without this, the agent dumps raw output or stays silent — neither is useful.
SKILL.md:1646:**Sidecar JSON:** Generate `quality/results/integration-results.json` by copying the template below verbatim and filling in only the values. Do not invent fields, rename keys, or restructure the schema. A flat list of commands without the `groups` array is **invalid** — even if the project runs all tests through a single command, wrap it in one group.

SKILL.md:1682:**Required top-level fields:** `schema_version`, `skill_version`, `date`, `project`, `recommendation`, `groups`, `summary`, `uc_coverage`. If any of these fields are missing from your output, the result is non-conformant.
SKILL.md:1686:- A schema with `"commands_run"` instead of `"groups"` — wrong key name.
SKILL.md:1687:- A schema missing `"uc_coverage"` — every use case from REQUIREMENTS.md must appear.
SKILL.md:1690:Valid `result` values: `"pass"`, `"fail"`, `"skipped"`, `"error"`. Valid `recommendation` values: `"SHIP"` (all groups pass), `"FIX BEFORE MERGE"` (failures in non-blocking groups), `"BLOCK"` (failures in critical groups). The `uc_coverage` section maps every use case from REQUIREMENTS.md to one of: `"covered_pass"` (at least one mapped group passed), `"covered_fail"` (groups mapped but all failed), or `"not_mapped"` (no integration test group maps to this use case). The distinction between `"covered_fail"` and `"not_mapped"` matters: the first means the test exists but the code is broken; the second means the test is missing.
SKILL.md:1694:**Post-write validation (mandatory).** After writing `integration-results.json`, reopen the file and verify: (1) every required top-level field is present, (2) every `groups[]` entry has `group`, `name`, `use_cases`, `result`, and `notes`, (3) all `result` and `recommendation` values use only the allowed enum values listed above, (4) `uc_coverage` maps every use case from REQUIREMENTS.md, (5) no extra undocumented root keys exist. If any check fails, fix the file before proceeding.
SKILL.md:1983:4. **Reviewer identifier stability.** Use fixed strings like `"claude-opus-4.7"`, `"gpt-5.4"`, `"gemini-2.5-pro"`. The majority computation in schemas.md §10 invariant #17 groups on this field — a typo silently becomes a fourth reviewer and breaks the 2-of-3 majority check.
SKILL.md:1987:**Majority rule (gate-enforced).** For each Tier 1/2 REQ, the gate groups reviews by `req_id` and fails the run when ≥2 of 3 reviewers recorded `verdict == "overreaches"`. A single-member `overreaches` or `unclear` verdict surfaces as a warning but does not fail the gate. A REQ with fewer than three reviewer entries (missing reviewer, skipped batch) has insufficient evidence — the gate treats that as a fail.
SKILL.md:2126:**Sidecar JSON post-write validation (mandatory).** After writing `quality/results/tdd-results.json` and/or `quality/results/integration-results.json`, immediately reopen each file and verify it contains all required keys. For `tdd-results.json`, the required root keys are: `schema_version`, `skill_version`, `date`, `project`, `bugs`, `summary`. Each entry in `bugs` must have: `id`, `requirement`, `red_phase`, `green_phase`, `verdict`, `fix_patch_present`, `writeup_path`. The `summary` object must include `confirmed_open` alongside `verified`, `red_failed`, `green_failed`. For `integration-results.json`, the required root keys are: `schema_version`, `skill_version`, `date`, `project`, `recommendation`, `groups`, `summary`, `uc_coverage`. Both files must have `schema_version: "1.1"`. If any key is missing, add it now — do not leave a non-conformant JSON file on disk. This validation exists because v1.3.25 benchmarking showed 6 of 8 repos with non-conformant sidecar JSON: httpx invented an alternate schema, serde used legacy shape, javalin omitted `summary` and per-bug fields, and others used invalid enum values.

quality/previous_runs/20260428-170027-PARTIAL/quality/EXPLORATION.md:232:### QR-7 [MEDIUM] — Integration recommendation enum accepts empty recommendation as "canonical"
quality/previous_runs/20260428-170027-PARTIAL/quality/EXPLORATION.md:234:`quality_gate.py:680–686` — when `recommendation` is empty string, the check goes into the `fail("recommendation missing")` branch. But `get_str(data, key)` at line 83–88 returns `""` for non-string values, so a `null` recommendation in JSON (which is likely during iteration drafts) also lands in the fail branch (correct). However, the check `if rec in ("SHIP", "FIX BEFORE MERGE", "BLOCK")` uses exact-match — a recommendation of `"ship"` (lowercase, which some models emit) is flagged non-canonical but the agent's *spelling* of the intent is correct. Same story for `result` in `groups[]` (line 689–700) and `uc_coverage` values (line 703–713). Any stringified boolean or differently-cased label is a fail. This is strict-by-design but punishes case-insensitive model output.

quality/previous_runs/20260416-160301/quality/BUGS.md:164:**Description**: The recheck mode JSON artifact template at SKILL.md line 1965 uses `"schema_version": "1.0"` while all other sidecar JSON artifacts (tdd-results.json at line 128, integration-results.json at line 156) use `"schema_version": "1.1"`. There is no migration note or documentation explaining why recheck uses a different schema version. `quality_gate.sh` does not validate `recheck-results.json` at all (confirmed by grep — no `recheck-results` in the gate script), so there is no mechanical enforcement of conformance. From a user perspective: (1) if a future gate version adds recheck validation, it must handle a different schema version than all other artifacts; (2) agents generating recheck output see a "1.0" example in the context of a skill that otherwise uses "1.1" everywhere, creating ambiguity.
quality/previous_runs/20260416-160301/quality/BUGS.md:277:**Description**: The integration test protocol template in `references/review_protocols.md` specifies the `recommendation` field as `[SHIP IT / FIX FIRST / NEEDS INVESTIGATION]` (line 410). This contradicts the canonical enum values established in `quality_gate.sh:427` (`SHIP|"FIX BEFORE MERGE"|BLOCK`) and `SKILL.md:1273` (`"SHIP"`, `"FIX BEFORE MERGE"`, `"BLOCK"`). An agent reading `references/review_protocols.md` (which is explicitly referenced by SKILL.md Phase 2's File 4 instructions as the source for integration test protocol generation) and following its template will write `"recommendation": "FIX FIRST"` or `"recommendation": "NEEDS INVESTIGATION"` into `integration-results.json`. The gate will then FAIL with: `recommendation 'FIX FIRST' is non-canonical (must be SHIP/FIX BEFORE MERGE/BLOCK)`. The old values are human-readable prose labels from when the integration protocol was a Markdown report; the canonical values are the machine-readable enum values required by the JSON schema. The reference file was not updated when the schema was formalized.

**Sidecar JSON:** Generate `quality/results/integration-results.json` by copying the template below verbatim and filling in only the values. Do not invent fields, rename keys, or restructure the schema. A flat list of commands without the `groups` array is **invalid** — even if the project runs all tests through a single command, wrap it in one group.

```json
{
  "schema_version": "1.1",
  "skill_version": "<current skill version>",
  "date": "YYYY-MM-DD",
  "project": "<project name>",
  "recommendation": "SHIP",
  "groups": [
    {
      "group": 1,
      "name": "Core routing dispatch",
      "use_cases": ["UC-01", "UC-02"],
      "result": "pass",
      "tests_passed": 5,
      "tests_failed": 0,
      "junit_file": "integration-group-1.xml",
      "junit_available": true,
      "notes": ""
    }
  ],
  "summary": {
    "total_groups": 9,
    "passed": 8,
    "failed": 1,
    "skipped": 0
  },
  "uc_coverage": {
    "UC-01": "covered_pass",
    "UC-02": "covered_pass",
    "UC-03": "not_mapped"
  }
}
```

**Required top-level fields:** `schema_version`, `skill_version`, `date`, `project`, `recommendation`, `groups`, `summary`, `uc_coverage`. If any of these fields are missing from your output, the result is non-conformant.

**Invalid examples (do not emit these):**
- A flat `"results": [{"command": "go test ./...", "result": "pass"}]` — this is not the grouped schema.
- A schema with `"commands_run"` instead of `"groups"` — wrong key name.
- A schema missing `"uc_coverage"` — every use case from REQUIREMENTS.md must appear.
- A schema with `"use_case_traceability"` instead of `"use_cases"` — wrong field name.

Valid `result` values: `"pass"`, `"fail"`, `"skipped"`, `"error"`. Valid `recommendation` values: `"SHIP"` (all groups pass), `"FIX BEFORE MERGE"` (failures in non-blocking groups), `"BLOCK"` (failures in critical groups). The `uc_coverage` section maps every use case from REQUIREMENTS.md to one of: `"covered_pass"` (at least one mapped group passed), `"covered_fail"` (groups mapped but all failed), or `"not_mapped"` (no integration test group maps to this use case). The distinction between `"covered_fail"` and `"not_mapped"` matters: the first means the test exists but the code is broken; the second means the test is missing.

Runner scripts and CI tools should read the sidecar JSON for results rather than grepping the Markdown report. This eliminates the class of bugs where grep-based counting produces wrong numbers from matching words in prose.

**Post-write validation (mandatory).** After writing `integration-results.json`, reopen the file and verify: (1) every required top-level field is present, (2) every `groups[]` entry has `group`, `name`, `use_cases`, `result`, and `notes`, (3) all `result` and `recommendation` values use only the allowed enum values listed above, (4) `uc_coverage` maps every use case from REQUIREMENTS.md, (5) no extra undocumented root keys exist. If any check fails, fix the file before proceeding.

**This protocol must exercise real external dependencies.** If the project talks to APIs, databases, or external services, the integration test protocol runs real end-to-end executions against those services — not just local validation checks. Design the test matrix around the project's actual execution modes and external dependencies. Look for API keys, provider abstractions, and existing integration test scripts during exploration and build on them.

**Derive quality gates from the code, not generic checks.** Read validation rules, schema enums, and generation logic during exploration. Turn them into per-pipeline quality checks with specific fields and acceptable value ranges. "All units validated" is not enough — the protocol must verify domain-specific correctness.

# Integration Test Protocol: QPB
> Generated by [Quality Playbook](https://github.com/andrewstellman/quality-playbook) v1.5.6 — Andrew Stellman
> Date: 2026-05-08 · Project: QPB

This run's integration surface is repo-local rather than networked: the goal is to validate end-to-end behavior across documentation discovery, ingest, bootstrap mirroring, phase gating, archive rendering, and role-map normalization using realistic temporary repository layouts.

## Read First

1. `quality/QUALITY.md`
2. `quality/REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `quality/test_functional.py`
4. `quality/EXPLORATION.md`

## Pre-flight

Run from the repo root:

```bash
python3 -m py_compile quality/test_functional.py
python3 -m unittest quality.test_functional.BoundaryAndEdgeCaseTests -v
```

If `py_compile` fails, stop and fix syntax/import issues before running any integration group.

## Integration groups

### Group A — Documentation-state parity

Goal: verify that docs detection, warning logic, and state evaluation agree across realistic repo layouts.

Command:

```bash
python3 -m unittest \
  quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_001_treats_cite_only_docs_as_docs_backed_input \
  quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_002_uses_one_recognized_docs_predicate -v
```

Expected focus:

- cite-only tree
- README-only tree
- binary-only tree
- top-level plaintext tree

### Group B — Tier 4 ingest and bootstrap mirroring

Goal: verify that the docs surface consumed by Phase 1 matches the docs surface created by the bootstrap helper.

Command:

```bash
python3 -m unittest \
  quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_003_limits_tier4_ingest_to_top_level_plaintext \
  quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_004_preserves_citable_vs_context_placement -v
```

### Group C — Phase boundary integrity

Goal: verify that malformed exploration artifacts and raw role maps do not slip through the mechanical boundary unchecked.

Command:

```bash
python3 -m unittest \
  quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_005_enforces_the_full_phase1_gate_mechanically \
  quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_007_normalizes_direct_phase1_role_maps -v
```

### Group D — Archive and inventory integrity

Goal: verify that archived findings and historical `quality/` output are represented accurately.

Command:

```bash
python3 -m unittest \
  quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_006_parses_canonical_titled_bug_headings \
  quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_008_keeps_playbook_output_out_of_intrinsic_surface_accounting -v
```

## Field Reference Table

| Group | Primary REQs | Primary UCs | Main code paths | Result shape |
|-------|--------------|-------------|-----------------|--------------|
| A | `REQ-001`, `REQ-002` | `UC-01`, `UC-02` | `bin/run_playbook.py` | pass/fail per repo layout |
| B | `REQ-003`, `REQ-004` | `UC-03`, `UC-04` | `bin/reference_docs_ingest.py`, `bin/bootstrap_self_audit_docs.py` | pass/fail per ingest/mirror surface |
| C | `REQ-005`, `REQ-007` | `UC-05`, `UC-07` | `bin/run_state_lib.py`, `bin/role_map.py` | pass/fail per phase-boundary artifact |
| D | `REQ-006`, `REQ-008` | `UC-06`, `UC-08` | `bin/archive_lib.py`, `bin/role_map.py` | pass/fail per summary/accounting rule |

## Recording results

Write the Phase 4 execution results to `quality/results/integration-results.json` with schema version `1.1`.

Use this root shape:

```json
{
  "schema_version": "1.1",
  "skill_version": "1.5.6",
  "date": "2026-05-08",
  "project": "QPB",
  "recommendation": "pass | investigate | fail",
  "groups": [],
  "summary": {},
  "uc_coverage": {}
}
```

Each executed group should record:

- command
- mapped REQs
- mapped UCs
- pass/fail status
- failing test names
- concise notes on any mismatches

## Exit criteria

- Every group has been executed or explicitly marked `NOT_RUN`.
- Any failure is traced back to one or more REQs.
- `uc_coverage` includes `UC-01` through `UC-08`.
- No result claims "pass" while the mapped tests actually fail.

  1560	def docs_present(repo_dir: Path) -> bool:
  1561	    # v1.5.5 BUG-006 follow-up: the canonical operator-supplied docs
  1562	    # directory is `reference_docs/` (this is what
  1563	    # bin/reference_docs_ingest.py reads). The pre-v1.5.2 layout used
  1564	    # `docs_gathered/`, which is still recognized as a fallback so
  1565	    # archived benchmarks with that layout don't suddenly report
  1566	    # "code-only" — but operator-facing prose now routes new users to
  1567	    # `reference_docs/` exclusively.
  1568	    for name in ("reference_docs", "docs_gathered"):
  1569	        docs_dir = repo_dir / name
  1570	        if not docs_dir.is_dir():
  1571	            continue
  1572	        for f in docs_dir.iterdir():
  1573	            if f.is_file() and not f.name.startswith(".") and f.stat().st_size > 0:
  1574	                return True
  1575	    return False
  1576

  1585	    if not reference_docs_dir.is_dir():
  1586	        return []

  1589	        if not path.is_file():
  1590	            continue
  1591	        if path.name in _REFERENCE_DOCS_SKIPPED:
  1592	            continue
  1593	        if path.suffix.lower() in _REFERENCE_DOCS_PLAINTEXT_EXTS:
  1594	            files.append(path)
  1595	    return files

    68	_VERSION_HEADER_PATTERN = re.compile(r"Quality Playbook v([0-9]+(?:\.[0-9]+)+)", re.IGNORECASE)
    69	_BUG_HEADING_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^###\s+BUG-([A-Za-z0-9]+)\s*$", re.MULTILINE)
    70	_REQ_HEADING_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^###\s+REQ-([A-Za-z0-9]+)", re.MULTILINE)
    71	_SEVERITY_PATTERN = re.compile(r"\*\*Severity\*\*\s*[:.-]\s*(HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW)", re.IGNORECASE)
    72	_PHASE_CHECK_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^-\s*\[x\]\s*Phase\s*([0-9a-zA-Z]+)", re.MULTILINE)
    73	_GATE_RESULT_PATTERN = re.compile(r"gate_result['\"]?\s*[:=]\s*['\"](PASS|FAIL|WARN)['\"]?", re.IGNORECASE)
    74	
   321	def _extract_bug_counts(run_folder: Path) -> Dict[str, int]:
   322	    text = _read_text(run_folder / "quality" / "BUGS.md") or _read_text(run_folder / "BUGS.md")
   323	    counts: Dict[str, int] = {level: 0 for level in SEVERITY_LITERALS}
   324	    counts.update({d: 0 for d in DISPOSITION_LITERALS})
   325	    if not text:
   326	        return counts
   327	    for segment in _split_by_heading(text, _BUG_HEADING_PATTERN):
   328	        sev_match = _SEVERITY_PATTERN.search(segment)
   329	        if sev_match:
   330	            counts[sev_match.group(1).upper()] += 1
   331	        for disposition in DISPOSITION_LITERALS:
   332	            if re.search(
   333	                rf"\*\*disposition\*\*\s*[:.-]\s*{re.escape(disposition)}",
   334	                segment,
   335	                re.IGNORECASE,
   336	            ):
   337	                counts[disposition] += 1
   338	    return counts
   339

    90	def _iter_candidates(root: Path) -> Iterable[Path]:
    91	    if not root.exists():
    92	        return []
    93	    return sorted(p for p in root.rglob("*") if p.is_file())

    96	def _rel(path: Path, target_repo: Path) -> str:
    97	    return str(path.relative_to(target_repo)).replace("\\", "/")
    98

   100	def _is_under_cite(path: Path, cite_dir: Path) -> bool:
   189	def _collect(target_repo: Path) -> List[_FileRecord]:
   190	    ref_dir = target_repo / REFERENCE_DIR_NAME
   191	    cite_dir = ref_dir / CITE_DIR_NAME
   192	    records: List[_FileRecord] = []
   193	
   194	    for path in _iter_candidates(ref_dir):
   195	        if path.name in SKIPPED_FILENAMES:
   196	            continue
   197	        # Phase 3.9.1 BUG 2 (surfaced during the 2026-04-30 empirical
   198	        # bootstrap test): skip dotfiles (.gitkeep, .DS_Store, etc.)
   199	        # before the extension gate. They're sentinel placeholders
   200	        # protecting otherwise-empty tracked directories — not
   201	        # citable content. Without this skip, .gitkeep reaches the
   202	        # extension check with suffix='' and raises IngestError
   203	        # ("unsupported extension ''"), aborting Phase 1 ingest.
   204	        if path.name.startswith("."):
   205	            continue
   206	        ext = path.suffix.lower()
   207	        if ext not in SUPPORTED_EXTENSIONS:
   208	            hint = REJECT_GUIDANCE.get(
   209	                ext, "Only .txt and .md are ingested — convert to plaintext first."
   210	            )
   211	            raise IngestError(f"{_rel(path, target_repo)}: unsupported extension '{ext}'. {hint}")
   212	        text = _read_text(path)
   213	        is_cite = _is_under_cite(path, cite_dir)
   214	        if is_cite:
   215	            tier = _parse_tier_marker(text)
   216	        else:
   217	            tier = 4
   218	        records.append(
   219	            _FileRecord(
   220	                path=path,
   221	                rel_path=_rel(path, target_repo),
   222	                text=text,
   223	                tier=tier,
   224	                is_cite=is_cite,
   225	            )
   226	        )
   227	    return records

   263	def load_tier4_context(target_repo: Path) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
   264	    """Return Tier 4 context as ``(rel_path, text)`` tuples, sorted by path.
   265	
   266	    Preserves the shape of the legacy ``load_informal_docs`` so existing
   267	    Phase 1 prompts keep working unchanged.
   268	    """
   269	    target_repo = Path(target_repo)
   270	    try:
   271	        records = _collect(target_repo)
   272	    except IngestError as exc:
   273	        raise Tier4LoadError(str(exc)) from exc
   274	    return sorted(
   275	        (rec.rel_path, rec.text) for rec in records if not rec.is_cite
   276	    )

import sys, unittest
mod_name, test_name = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
module = __import__(mod_name, fromlist=['*'])
cls_name, meth_name = test_name.split('.')
cls = getattr(module, cls_name)
fn = getattr(cls, meth_name)
if getattr(fn, '__unittest_expecting_failure__', False):
    setattr(fn, '__unittest_expecting_failure__', False)
suite = unittest.defaultTestLoader.loadTestsFromName(f\"{mod_name}.{cls_name}.{meth_name}\")
res = unittest.TextTestRunner(verbosity=2).run(suite)
sys.exit(0 if res.wasSuccessful() else 1)
'''


def ignore(dir, entries):
    skipped = {'.git', '__pycache__', '.pytest_cache', 'metrics'}
    if Path(dir).name == 'quality':
        skipped.update({'previous_runs', 'runs', 'results', 'writeups', 'challenge'})
    return {e for e in entries if e in skipped}

    class_and_method = '.'.join(test_tail.split('.')[-2:]) if '.' in test_tail else 'CodeReviewRegressionTests.' + test_tail

/bin/zsh -lc "python3 - <<'PY'
from __future__ import annotations
import json, re, shutil, subprocess, sys, tempfile
from pathlib import Path

root = Path('.').resolve()
python = sys.executable

bugs = [
    ('BUG-001', 'REQ-001', 'CodeReviewRegressionTests', 'test_bug_001_docs_present_recognizes_cite_only_docs', 'quality/patches/BUG-001-fix.patch'),
    ('BUG-002', 'REQ-002', 'CodeReviewRegressionTests', 'test_bug_002_docs_present_uses_the_recognized_plaintext_predicate', 'quality/patches/BUG-002-fix.patch'),
    ('BUG-003', 'REQ-003', 'CodeReviewRegressionTests', 'test_bug_003_tier4_context_excludes_nested_non_cite_archives', 'quality/patches/BUG-003-fix.patch'),
    ('BUG-004', 'REQ-004', 'CodeReviewRegressionTests', 'test_bug_004_bootstrap_mirror_preserves_cite_subtree', 'quality/patches/BUG-004-fix.patch'),
    ('BUG-005', 'REQ-005', 'CodeReviewRegressionTests', 'test_bug_005_phase1_validator_enforces_the_written_gate', 'quality/patches/BUG-005-fix.patch'),
    ('BUG-006', 'REQ-006', 'CodeReviewRegressionTests', 'test_bug_006_archive_bug_count_accepts_titled_bug_headings', 'quality/patches/BUG-006-fix.patch'),
]

integration_groups = [
    (1, 'Documentation-state parity', ['UC-01', 'UC-02'], [
        'quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_001_treats_cite_only_docs_as_docs_backed_input',
        'quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_002_uses_one_recognized_docs_predicate',
    ]),
    (2, 'Tier 4 ingest and bootstrap mirroring', ['UC-03', 'UC-04'], [
        'quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_003_limits_tier4_ingest_to_top_level_plaintext',
        'quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_004_preserves_citable_vs_context_placement',
    ]),
    (3, 'Phase boundary integrity', ['UC-05', 'UC-07'], [
        'quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_005_enforces_the_full_phase1_gate_mechanically',
        'quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_007_normalizes_direct_phase1_role_maps',
    ]),
    (4, 'Archive and inventory integrity', ['UC-06', 'UC-08'], [
        'quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_006_parses_canonical_titled_bug_headings',
        'quality.test_functional.SpecRequirementTests.test_req_008_keeps_playbook_output_out_of_intrinsic_surface_accounting',
    ]),
]

runner_code = r'''
import sys, unittest
mod_name, cls_name, meth_name = sys.argv[1:4]
module = __import__(mod_name, fromlist=['*'])
cls = getattr(module, cls_name)
fn = getattr(cls, meth_name)
if getattr(fn, '__unittest_expecting_failure__', False):
    setattr(fn, '__unittest_expecting_failure__', False)
suite = unittest.defaultTestLoader.loadTestsFromName(f\"{mod_name}.{cls_name}.{meth_name}\")
res = unittest.TextTestRunner(verbosity=2).run(suite)
sys.exit(0 if res.wasSuccessful() else 1)
'''

def make_repo(tmp_root: Path) -> Path:
    repo = tmp_root / 'repo'
    repo.mkdir()
    shutil.copytree(root / 'bin', repo / 'bin')
    (repo / 'quality').mkdir()
    shutil.copy2(root / 'quality' / 'test_regression.py', repo / 'quality' / 'test_regression.py')
    shutil.copytree(root / 'quality' / 'patches', repo / 'quality' / 'patches')
    return repo

results = []
for bug_id, req, cls_name, meth_name, patch_rel in bugs:
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        repo = make_repo(Path(tmp))
        red = subprocess.run([python, '-c', runner_code, 'quality.test_regression', cls_name, meth_name], cwd=repo, text=True, capture_output=True)
        apply_res = subprocess.run(['git', 'apply', patch_rel], cwd=repo, text=True, capture_output=True)
        green = subprocess.run([python, '-c', runner_code, 'quality.test_regression', cls_name, meth_name], cwd=repo, text=True, capture_output=True)
        results.append({
            'id': bug_id,
            'requirement': req,
            'red_code': red.returncode,
            'green_code': green.returncode,
            'apply_code': apply_res.returncode,
            'red_stdout': red.stdout,
            'red_stderr': red.stderr,
            'green_stdout': green.stdout,
            'green_stderr': green.stderr,
            'apply_stdout': apply_res.stdout,
            'apply_stderr': apply_res.stderr,
        })

ig_results = []
for group, name, ucs, tests in integration_groups:
    cmd = [python, '-m', 'unittest', *tests, '-v']
    run = subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=root, text=True, capture_output=True)
    combined = (run.stdout or '') + ('\\n' if run.stdout and run.stderr else '') + (run.stderr or '')
    m = re.search(r'Ran\\s+(\\d+)\\s+tests?', combined)
    ran = int(m.group(1)) if m else len(tests)
    failures = 0
    m2 = re.search(r'FAILED \\((.*?)\\)', combined)
    if m2:
        for part in m2.group(1).split(','):
            part = part.strip()
            if '=' in part:
                k, v = part.split('=', 1)
                if k.strip() in {'failures', 'errors'}:
                    failures += int(v.strip())
    tests_failed = failures
    tests_passed = max(ran - tests_failed, 0)
    result = 'pass' if run.returncode == 0 else 'fail'
    ig_results.append({
        'group': group,
        'name': name,
        'use_cases': ucs,
        'result': result,
        'tests_passed': tests_passed,
        'tests_failed': tests_failed,
        'stdout': run.stdout,
        'stderr': run.stderr,
        'returncode': run.returncode,
    })

print(json.dumps({'tdd': results, 'integration': ig_results}, indent=2))

## Phase 3 confirmation checklist

1. For every pattern-tagged REQ, I produced a compensation grid in `quality/compensation_grid.json`.
2. For every grid, I applied the BUG-default rule mechanically.
3. Every BUG emitted for a pattern-tagged REQ has a `- Covers: [...]` field with valid cell IDs.
4. Every BUG whose Covers list has ≥2 entries has a non-empty `- Consolidation rationale: ...` field.
5. For every downgraded cell, I wrote a complete structured record in `quality/compensation_grid_downgrades.json` with all five required fields and a valid `reason_class`. This run has zero downgraded cells, so the file contains an explicit empty `downgrades` list.
6. For every pattern-tagged REQ, the union of Covers lists + downgrade cells equals the grid's absent-cell set.

# Write writeups.
header = f'> Generated by [Quality Playbook](https://github.com/andrewstellman/quality-playbook) {skill_version} — Andrew Stellman\\n> Date: {date} · Project: {project}\\n'
for bug_id, meta in bugs.items():
    sections = [
        f'# {bug_id} Writeup',
        header.rstrip(),
        '',
        '## Summary',
        meta['summary'],
        '',
        '## Spec reference',
        f\"- Requirement: {meta['requirement']}\",
        f\"- Spec basis: {meta['spec_basis']}\",
        f\"- Behavioral contract quote: \\\"{meta['quote']}\\\"\",
        '',
        '## The code',
        f\"At {meta['location']}, {meta['code']}\",
        '',
        '## Observable consequence',
        meta['consequence'],
        '',
    ]
    if 'depth' in meta:
        sections.extend(['## Depth judgment', meta['depth'], ''])
    sections.extend([
        '## The fix',
        meta['fix_explainer'],
        '"'```diff'"',
        patch_diffs[bug_id],
        '"'```'"',
        '',
        '## The test',
        f\"Running "'`python3 -m unittest quality.test_regression.CodeReviewRegressionTests.{meta['"'test'].strip('"'`'"').split('.')[-1]}"'` against the current source fails in a disposable temp copy, and applying `{meta['"'fix_patch'].strip('"'`'"')}"'` in the same temp-copy harness makes the same regression test pass.",
        f"- Regression test: {meta['"'test']}\",
        f\"- Regression patch: {meta['reg_patch']}\",
        f\"- Fix patch: {meta['fix_patch']}\",
        f\"- Red receipt: "'`quality/results/{bug_id}.red.log`",
        f"- Green receipt: `quality/results/{bug_id}.green.log`",
    ])
    if meta['"'related']:
        sections.extend(['', '## Related issues'])
        sections.extend(meta['related'])
    sections.append('')
    (writeups / f'{bug_id}.md').write_text('\\n'.join(sections), encoding='utf-8')

# TDD traceability.
trace_lines = [
    '# TDD Traceability',
    header.rstrip(),
    '',
    '| Bug ID | Requirement ID | Spec Doc | Spec Section | Behavioral Contract | Test File:Function | Red Result | Green Result |',
    '|--------|----------------|----------|--------------|---------------------|--------------------|------------|--------------|',
]
for bug_id, meta in bugs.items():
    test_fn = meta['test'].strip('"'`'"')
    req_range = meta['spec_basis'].strip('"'`'"').split(':', 1)[1]
    trace_lines.append(
        f\"| {bug_id} | {meta['requirement']} | quality/REQUIREMENTS.md | {req_range} | {meta['quote']} | {test_fn} | FAIL | PASS |\"
    )
trace_lines.append('')
(quality / 'TDD_TRACEABILITY.md').write_text('\\n'.join(trace_lines), encoding='utf-8')

# BUG-006 Writeup
> Generated by [Quality Playbook](https://github.com/andrewstellman/quality-playbook) v1.5.6 — Andrew Stellman
> Date: 2026-05-08 · Project: QPB

## Summary
_extract_bug_counts() ignores canonical `### BUG-NNN: Title` headings, so archive severity totals undercount real bugs.

## Spec reference
- Requirement: REQ-006
- Spec basis: `quality/REQUIREMENTS.md:119-129`
- Behavioral contract quote: "count bugs from the canonical heading form `### BUG-NNN: Title`"

## The code
At `bin/archive_lib.py:69`, `bin/archive_lib.py:321-338`, At `bin/archive_lib.py:69`, `_BUG_HEADING_PATTERN` matches only bare `### BUG-NNN` lines. `_extract_bug_counts()` at `bin/archive_lib.py:321-338` uses that pattern to split `BUGS.md` into segments before it counts severities, so canonical titled headings are skipped before the severity parser ever runs.

## Observable consequence
If an archived run contains a canonical heading such as `### BUG-001: Cite-only docs trigger false code-only warning`, `_extract_bug_counts()` reports zero HIGH bugs even though the bug section and severity line are present. Archive indexes and summaries then under-report the defect count for the run.

## The fix
Allowing an optional `: Title` suffix in `_BUG_HEADING_PATTERN` makes the archive parser accept the same canonical heading form that the rest of the playbook already requires.
```diff
--- a/bin/archive_lib.py
+++ b/bin/archive_lib.py
@@ -66,7 +66,7 @@
 PARTIAL_SENTINEL_NAME = ".partial"
 
 _VERSION_HEADER_PATTERN = re.compile(r"Quality Playbook v([0-9]+(?:\.[0-9]+)+)", re.IGNORECASE)
-_BUG_HEADING_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^###\s+BUG-([A-Za-z0-9]+)\s*$", re.MULTILINE)
+_BUG_HEADING_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^###\s+BUG-([A-Za-z0-9]+)(?::\s+.+)?\s*$", re.MULTILINE)
 _REQ_HEADING_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^###\s+REQ-([A-Za-z0-9]+)", re.MULTILINE)
 _SEVERITY_PATTERN = re.compile(r"\*\*Severity\*\*\s*[:.-]\s*(HIGH|MEDIUM|LOW)", re.IGNORECASE)
 _PHASE_CHECK_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^-\s*\[x\]\s*Phase\s*([0-9a-zA-Z]+)", re.MULTILINE)
```

## The test
Running `python3 -m unittest quality.test_regression.CodeReviewRegressionTests.test_bug_006_archive_bug_count_accepts_titled_bug_headings` against the current source fails in a disposable temp copy, and applying `quality/patches/BUG-006-fix.patch` in the same temp-copy harness makes the same regression test pass.
- Regression test: `quality.test_regression.CodeReviewRegressionTests.test_bug_006_archive_bug_count_accepts_titled_bug_headings`
- Regression patch: `quality/patches/BUG-006-regression-test.patch`
- Fix patch: `quality/patches/BUG-006-fix.patch`
- Red receipt: `quality/results/BUG-006.red.log`
- Green receipt: `quality/results/BUG-006.green.log`

## Testability audit

- `REQ-001` is concretely testable with a tempfile repo containing only `reference_docs/cite/spec.md`.
- `REQ-002` is concretely testable with README-only, PDF-only, and plaintext-top-level temp trees.
- `REQ-003` is concretely testable with top-level and nested `reference_docs/` fixtures and a direct call to `load_tier4_context()`.
- `REQ-004` is concretely testable by patching bootstrap source/destination paths and asserting post-copy placement.
- `REQ-005` is concretely testable with a malformed 120-line `EXPLORATION.md` fixture and a direct call to `validate_phase_artifacts(..., 1)`.
- `REQ-006` is concretely testable with a temporary archived `BUGS.md` using canonical titled headings.
- `REQ-007` is concretely testable with a raw role-map JSON fixture normalized through `normalize_role_map_for_gate()`.
- `REQ-008` is concretely testable with a synthetic `files[]` list passed to `compute_breakdown()`.

Result: every Phase 2 requirement in this baseline has at least one direct executable test shape, and every QUALITY.md scenario maps to at least one named test in `quality/test_functional.py`.

## Cross-requirement consistency

- `REQ-001` and `REQ-002` are intentionally coupled: `REQ-001` defines the cite-only edge case, while `REQ-002` requires the helper surfaces to agree across all document shapes. These are consistent and non-duplicative.
- `REQ-003` and `REQ-004` are complementary: one constrains what ingest may read, the other constrains how bootstrap mirroring must populate that surface.
- `REQ-005` and `REQ-007` both concern phase boundaries, but they target different artifacts (`EXPLORATION.md` vs. `exploration_role_map.json`) and do not conflict.
- `REQ-006` and `REQ-008` both protect reporting accuracy, but one is parser-format correctness and the other is accounting isolation; no contradiction was found.

- No Phase 3 compensation grids were generated yet. Pattern tags are preserved on the affected REQs so the review phase can build the required grids if any pattern-driven bug is confirmed.

---
name: quality-playbook
description: "Run a complete quality engineering audit on any codebase. Derives behavioral requirements from the code, generates spec-traced functional tests, runs a three-pass code review with regression tests, executes a multi-model spec audit (Council of Three), and produces a consolidated bug report with TDD-verified patches. Finds the 35% of real defects that structural code review alone cannot catch. Works with any language. Trigger on 'quality playbook', 'spec audit', 'Council of Three', 'fitness-to-purpose', or 'coverage theater'."

BUG-006.md
